# MIDAS RIN coverage — gap analysis

_Auto-generated by `scripts/build-midas-coverage-report.py`. Do not edit by hand. Last run: 2026-09-23._

Each MIDAS RIN classified as:
- **covered-urpx** — a urpx:RatePlan or urpx:RatePlanModifier in this corpus semantically corresponds
- **covered-gridx** — the grid-coordination price server publishes this rate via GridX/CAISO feed
- **covered-both** — both
- **gap-{sector}** — neither URPX nor GridX covers; the sector (Residential, Commercial, Industrial, Agricultural, Lighting) suggests which path to closure

## How to read the gap counts

A `gap-*` row means no modeled schedule slug and no GridX feed code matched this identifier's RateName or AltRateName. Matching is a strict prefix comparison with a digit boundary, so it is quiet rather than wrong when a publisher does not name rates by schedule.

| Utility | Identifiers | Matched a modeled schedule | Publishes a Sector |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| PG&E | 629 | 150 | 618 |
| SCE | 759 | 24 | 759 |
| SDG&E | 4,009 | 598 | 4,009 |
| SMUD | 84 | 0 | 84 |
| LADWP | 3,478 | 10 | 0 |

Where the matched column is near zero the limit is naming, not modeling. LADWP publishes no Sector on any identifier and its RINs enumerate load-factor and power-factor cells rather than schedules, so its count is not comparable to a schedule count. SMUD names residential rates in forms the prefix rule cannot reach: `USCA-SMSM-TOD5-0000` carries RateName "Time-of-Day 5-8PM", which is this corpus's `tod-5-8pm`, spelled out rather than as the schedule code. That is one known instance, left unmatched here rather than bridged by an alias table.
